\displaystyle\ \underbrace{1[t\in\mathcal{A}_{n}]|\psi_{0}^{\prime}(t)|g_{0}(t)\frac{|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(t)|}{\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})}}_{b_{1n}(t)}+\underbrace{1[t\in\mathcal{A}_{n}]|\psi_{0}^{\prime}(t)|g_{0}(t)\frac{|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(t+\tilde{\delta}_{n})|}{\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})}}_{b_{2n}(t)}.

Now defining

T_{21,n}(t)=1[t\in\mathcal{A}_{n}]|\psi_{0}^{\prime}(t)|\sqrt{g_{0}(t)}\bigg(\sqrt{g_{0}(t)}-\sqrt{\tilde{g}_{n}(t)}\bigg)\frac{|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(t)|}{\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})},

T_{22,n}(t)=1[t\in\mathcal{A}_{n}]|\psi_{0}^{\prime}(t)|\sqrt{g_{0}(t)}\frac{|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(t)|\sqrt{\tilde{g}_{n}(t)}}{\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})},

T_{23,n}(t)=1[t\in\mathcal{A}_{n}]|\psi_{0}^{\prime}(t)|\frac{{g_{0}(t)}}{\sqrt{g_{0}(t+\tilde{\delta}_{n})}}\bigg(\sqrt{g_{0}(t+\tilde{\delta}_{n})}-\sqrt{\tilde{g}_{n}(t+\tilde{\delta}_{n})}\bigg)\frac{|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(t+\tilde{\delta}_{n})|}{\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})},

T_{24,n}(t)=1[t\in\mathcal{A}_{n}]|\psi_{0}^{\prime}(t)|\frac{{g_{0}(t)}}{\sqrt{g_{0}(t+\tilde{\delta}_{n})}}\frac{|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(t+\tilde{\delta}_{n})|\sqrt{\tilde{g}_{n}(t+\tilde{\delta}_{n})}}{\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})},

we note that

b_{1n}(t)=T_{21,n}(t)+T_{22,n}(t)\quad\text{and}\quad b_{2n}(t)=T_{23,n}(t)+T_{24,n}(t).

Thus we can upper bound
|b_{n}(t)|
by
c_{n}(t)
where

c_{n}(t)=T_{21,n}(t)+T_{22,n}(t)+T_{23,n}(t)+T_{24,n}(t).

Our aim is to apply Fact
10
(Pratt’s Lemma) with
a_{n}=0
to prove the current Lemma. To this end, we first show that the following assertions hold on
\mathcal{M}
:

A1.

T_{21,n}\to_{n}0
and
T_{23,n}\to_{n}0
Lebesgue almost everywhere on
\mathbb{R}
.

A2.

\int_{\mathbb{R}}T_{21,n}(t)dt\to_{n}0
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}}T_{23,n}(t)dt\to_{n}0
.

A3.

There are functions
t_{22}:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
and
t_{24}:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
so that
T_{22}\to_{n}t_{22}
and
T_{24}\to_{n}t_{24}(t)
Lebesgue almost everywhere on
\mathbb{R}
.

A4.

The functions
t_{22}
and
t_{24}
in A3 are integrable. Moreover,
\int_{\mathbb{R}}T_{22,n}(t)dt\to_{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}}t_{22}(t)dt
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}}T_{24,n}(t)dt\to_{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}}t_{24}(t)dt
.

Let us denote
c(t)=t_{22}(t)+t_{24}(t)
. Then
A1-A4 imply that on
\mathcal{M}
,
c_{n}(t)\to_{n}c(t)
Lebesgue almost everywhere,
c
is integrable, and

\int_{\mathbb{R}}c_{n}(t)dt\to_{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}}c(t)dt
. Since
|b_{n}(t)|\leq c_{n}(t)
and (
34
) holds, Pratt’s Lemma (see Fact
10
) yields

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}b_{n}(t)dt\to_{n}\displaystyle\int_{G_{0}^{-1}(0)}^{G_{0}^{-1}(1)}\frac{g_{0}^{\prime}(t)^{2}\psi_{0}^{\prime}(t)dt}{\mathcal{I}_{f_{0}}}=1\quad\text{on }\mathcal{M},

which completes the proof of Lemma
A.1
.

Proof of A1 and A3:

Since
\|\tilde{g}_{n}-g_{0}\|_{\infty}\to_{n}0
on
\mathcal{M}
, Lemma
A.8
implies that on
\mathcal{M}
, the functions
\tilde{g}_{n},\tilde{g}_{n}(\mathord{\cdot}+\tilde{\delta}_{n})
converge pointwise to
g_{0}
, and
\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n},\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(\mathord{\cdot}+\tilde{\delta}_{n})
converge to
\psi_{0}^{\prime}
Lebesgue almost everywhere on
\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0}))
. Continuity of
g_{0}
implies
g_{0}(t+\tilde{\delta}_{n})\to_{n}g_{0}(t)
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Using the above, it can be shown that

T_{21,n},T_{23,n}\to_{n}0,\quad T_{22,n},T_{24,n}\to_{n}1_{\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0}))}{\psi_{0}^{\prime 2}g_{0}}/{\mathcal{I}_{f_{0}}}\quad\text{a.e. Lebesgue}\ \text{on }\mathcal{M}.

Proof of A2:

Using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the bound on
g_{0}
from Fact
1
, and the bound on
\psi_{0}^{\prime}
from (
32
), we can show that there exists
C>0
such that the following holds for all sufficiently large
n
on
\mathcal{M}
:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|T_{21,n}(t)|dt\

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ C\log n\left(\displaystyle\int_{-\xi_{n}-\tilde{\delta}_{n}}^{\xi_{n}}\frac{\Big(\sqrt{g_{0}(t)}-\sqrt{\tilde{g}_{n}(t)}\Big)^{2}}{{\tilde{g}_{n}(t)}}\right)^{1/2}\bigg(\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\frac{|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(t)|^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(t)dt}{\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})^{2}}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ C\log n\frac{H(\tilde{g}_{n},g_{0})}{\Big(\inf_{x\in\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{n}}\tilde{g}_{n}(x)\Big)^{1/2}}\frac{\sqrt{\mathcal{I}_{1n}}}{\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})},

which approaches zero as
n\to\infty
because

\frac{C(\log n)H(g_{0},\tilde{g}_{n})}{\Big(\inf_{x\in\mathcal{A}_{n}^{\prime}}\tilde{g}_{n}(x)\Big)^{1/2}}\to_{n}0,\quad\mathcal{I}_{1n},\ \widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})\to_{n}\mathcal{I}_{f_{0}}\quad\text{on }\mathcal{M}.

(37)

by (
A.1
).
The proof for
T_{23,n}
is similar. An application of the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the bound on
\psi_{0}^{\prime}
by (
32
) and the bound in (
33
) imply that the following holds for all large
n
on
\mathcal{M}
:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}T_{23,n}(t)dt\leq

\displaystyle\ \frac{C(\log n)H(g_{0},\tilde{g}_{n})}{\Big(\inf_{x\in\mathcal{A}_{n}^{\prime}}\tilde{g}_{n}(x)\Big)^{1/2}}\frac{\bigg(\displaystyle\int_{-\xi_{n}+\tilde{\delta}_{n}}^{\xi_{n}}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(t)^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(t)dt\bigg)^{1/2}}{\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{C(\log n)H(g_{0},\tilde{g}_{n})}{\Big(\inf_{x\in\mathcal{A}_{n}^{\prime}}\tilde{g}_{n}(x)\Big)^{1/2}}\frac{\sqrt{\mathcal{I}_{2n}}}{\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})},

which converges to zero by (
37
) and the fact that
\mathcal{I}_{2n}\to_{n}\mathcal{I}_{f_{0}}
on
\mathcal{M}
,
thus completing the proof of A2.

Proof of A4:

Let us define

\mathcal{T}_{22,n}(t)=|T_{22,n}(t)|/g_{0}(t)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathcal{T}_{24,n}(t)=|T_{24,n}(t)|/g_{0}(t).

We will show that on
\mathcal{M}
,
for each
\epsilon>0
, there exists
\sigma>0
so that the following bounds are true for any
G_{0}
-measurable set
\mathcal{B}\subset\mathbb{R}
satisfying
\int_{\mathcal{B}}g_{0}(t)dt<\sigma
:

\displaystyle\limsup_{n}\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{T}_{22,n}(t)g_{0}(t)dt<\epsilon\quad\text{and}\quad\limsup_{n}\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{T}_{24,n}(t)g_{0}(t)dt<\epsilon.

(38)

Next, we will show that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathcal{T}_{22,n}(t)g_{0}(t)dt<\infty\ \text{and}\ \limsup_{n}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathcal{T}_{24,n}(t)g_{0}(t)dt<\infty.

(39)

If (
38
) and (
39
) hold, Fact
13

underscores that the sequences
(\mathcal{T}_{22,n})_{n\geq 1}
and
(\mathcal{T}_{24,n})_{n\geq 1}
are uniformly integrable with respect to the measure induced by
G_{0}
. Then A4 follows from A3 and Theorem 16.13 (pp. 220) of
Billingsley 1986
(Vitali convergence Theorem).
Thus it suffices to show that (
38
) and (
39
) hold.

Note that since
\mathcal{I}_{f_{0}}<\infty
, by Fact
12
, given any
\epsilon>0
, we can choose
\sigma>0
so that for any
G_{0}
-measurable set
\mathcal{B}\subset\mathbb{R}
satisfying
\int_{\mathcal{B}}g_{0}(t)dt<\sigma
, the integral

\int_{\mathcal{B}}\psi_{0}(x)^{2}g_{0}(x)<\epsilon^{2}\mathcal{I}_{f_{0}}=\epsilon^{\prime}
(say).
It will soon be clear why this choice of
\epsilon^{\prime}
works. Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in the third step, we calculate

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{T}_{22,n}(t)g_{0}(t)dt=

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}}T_{22,n}(t)dt=\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}\cap\mathcal{A}_{n}}|\psi_{0}^{\prime}(t)|\sqrt{g_{0}(t)}\frac{|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(t)|\sqrt{\tilde{g}_{n}(t)}}{\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \bigg(\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}}\psi_{0}^{\prime}(t)^{2}g_{0}(t)dt\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg(\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\frac{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(t)^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(t)dt}{\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})^{2}}\bigg)^{1/2},

which is bounded by
\sqrt{\epsilon^{\prime}\mathcal{I}_{1n}}/\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})
. Noting
\mathcal{I}_{1n}\to_{n}\mathcal{I}_{f_{0}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})\to_{n}\mathcal{I}_{f_{0}}
on
\mathcal{M}
, we obtain

\limsup_{n}\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{T}_{22,n}(t)g_{0}(t)dt\leq\sqrt{\epsilon^{\prime}/\mathcal{I}_{f_{0}}}=\epsilon\quad\text{on }\mathcal{M}.

Letting
\mathcal{B}=\mathbb{R}
, and repeating the above steps, we can show that

\limsup_{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{T}_{22,n}(t)g_{0}(t)dt<1\quad\text{ on }\mathcal{M}.

For
\mathcal{T}_{24,n}
, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality yields

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{T}_{24,n}(t)g_{0}(t)dt\leq

\displaystyle\ \bigg(\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}\cap\mathcal{A}_{n}}\frac{g_{0}(t)^{2}\psi_{0}^{\prime}(t)^{2}}{g_{0}(t+\tilde{\delta}_{n})}dt\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg(\displaystyle\int_{-\xi_{n}+\tilde{\delta}_{n}}^{\xi_{n}}\frac{\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(t)^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(t)dt}{\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})^{2}}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\ \sqrt{\epsilon^{\prime}}\sup_{t\in\mathcal{A}_{n}}\bigg(\frac{g_{0}(t)}{g_{0}(t+\tilde{\delta}_{n})}\bigg)^{1/2}\frac{\mathcal{I}_{2n}^{1/2}}{\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})}.

(40)

Here (a) follows because
\int_{\mathcal{B}}\psi^{\prime}_{0}(t)^{2}g_{0}(t)dt<\epsilon^{\prime}
.
The fact that
\mathcal{I}_{1n}
,
\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})\to_{n}\mathcal{I}_{f_{0}}
on
\mathcal{M}
, in conjunction with the bound in (
33
), implies

\limsup_{n}\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{T}_{24,n}(t)g_{0}(t)dt\leq\sqrt{\epsilon^{\prime}/\mathcal{I}_{f_{0}}}=\epsilon\quad\text{on }\mathcal{M}.

Thus (
38
) is proved. Letting
\mathcal{B}=\mathbb{R}
leads to
\limsup_{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{T}_{24,n}(t)g_{0}(t)dt<1
on
\mathcal{M}
, thus finishing the proof of (
39
).

∎

A.2
Auxilliary lemmas for the proof of Theorem
1

A.2.1
Lemmas on
\xi_{n}
:

Unless otherwise mentioned, for all the lemmas on
\xi_{n}
,
\xi_{n}
will denote
\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(1-\eta_{n})
, where the choice of
\tilde{g}_{n}
should be clear from the context.

Lemma A.2
.

Suppose
\tilde{g}_{n}\in\mathcal{S}_{0}
satisfies Conditions
1
and
2
.
Let
\eta_{n}=Cn^{-2p/5}
, where
C>0
and
p
is as in Condition
2
.
Then for
\xi_{n}=\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(1-\eta_{n})
, we have

P\Big([-\xi_{n},\xi_{n}]\subset\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0}))\Big)\to 1.

Proof of Lemma
A.2
.

Using Fact
5
in step (a) we obtain that

|G_{0}(-\xi_{n})-\tilde{G}_{n}(-\xi_{n})|\leq d_{TV}(G_{0},\tilde{G}_{n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{2}H(\tilde{g}_{n},g_{0})=O_{p}(n^{-p})

by Condition
2
.
Therefore
G_{0}(-\xi_{n})\geq\tilde{G}_{n}(-\xi_{n})+O_{p}(n^{-p})\geq\eta_{n}+O_{p}(n^{-p})
because
F(F^{-1}(q))\geq q
for any distribution function
F
, and
q\in(0,1)
. Since
\eta_{n}=Cn^{-2p/5}\gg n^{-p}
, it follows that
P(G_{0}(-\xi_{n})\geq\eta_{n}/2)\to 1
. Thus
P(-\xi_{n}\in\mathrm{int}(dom(\psi_{0})))\to_{n}1
. Since
\psi_{0}\in\mathcal{SC}_{0}
,
\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0}))
is an interval of the form
(-a,a)
for some
a>0
. Noting
-\xi_{n}\in(-a,a)
implies
[-\xi_{n},\xi_{n}]\subset(-a,a)
, the proof follows.

∎

Lemma A.3
.

Consider the set up of Lemma
A.2
. Then
\xi_{n}\to_{p}G_{0}^{-1}(1)
as
\eta_{n}\to 0
.

Proof of Lemma
A.3
.

This has been changed.

Suppose, if possible,

\xi_{n}\to_{p}G_{0}^{-1}(1)
does not hold. We will consider two cases then: (a)
G_{0}^{-1}(1)<\infty
and (b)
G_{0}^{-1}(1)=\infty
.

Case (a):

Since
\xi_{n}\to_{p}G_{0}^{-1}(1)
does not hold, we can find an
\epsilon>0
and a subsequence
\{n_{k}\}\subset\{n\}
so that
\liminf_{k}P(|\xi_{n_{k}}-G_{0}^{-1}(1)|>\epsilon)>0
. To avoid cumbersome notation, we will denote
\xi_{n_{k}}
by
\xi_{n}
from now on. Since Lemma
A.2
implies
P(\xi_{n}\leq G_{0}^{-1}(1))\to 1
, it follows that
\liminf_{n}P(\xi_{n}<G_{0}^{-1}(1)-\epsilon)>0
. Now we show that there exists some
t\in[1/2,,1)
such that
G_{0}^{-1}(t)=G_{0}^{-1}(1)-\epsilon
.

Let us denote
t=G_{0}(G_{0}^{-1}(1)-\epsilon)
.
Since
g_{0}
is log-concave, it is positive on
J(G_{0})
. Therefore, if
t,t^{\prime}\in J(G_{0})
satisfies
t<t^{\prime}
, then
G_{0}(t)<G_{0}(t^{\prime})
. Note that
\epsilon
can be chosen small enough so that
G_{0}^{-1}(1)-\epsilon\in(G_{0}^{-1}(1/2),G_{0}^{-1}(1))
,
which implies
t=G_{0}(G_{0}^{-1}(1)-\epsilon)>G_{0}(G_{0}^{-1}(1/2))\geq 1/2
. By our choice of
\epsilon
, the number
G_{0}^{-1}(1)-\epsilon\in[G_{0}^{-1}(1/2),G_{0}^{-1}(1))
. Because
G_{0}
is strictly increasing on the latter set, it can be seen that
t=G_{0}(G_{0}^{-1}(1)-\epsilon)<1
. Therefore,
t\in[1/2,1)
. Finally,

G_{0}^{-1}(t)=G_{0}^{-1}\left(G_{0}(G_{0}^{-1}(1)-\epsilon)\right)=G_{0}^{-1}(1)-\epsilon,

where the last step follows because
G_{0}^{-1}(1)-\epsilon\in[G_{0}^{-1}(1/2),G_{0}^{-1}(1))
implies we can find a neighborhood of
G_{0}^{-1}(1)-\epsilon
where
G_{0}
is strictly increasing. Therefore, we have
proved that there exists
t\in[1/2,1)
so that
G_{0}^{-1}(t)=G_{0}^{-1}(1)-\epsilon
, which yields

\liminf_{n}P(\xi_{n}<G_{0}^{-1}(t))>0.

However, because
\eta_{n}\to 0
,
1-\eta_{n}\geq(1+t)/2
for sufficiently large
n
, which yields

\xi_{n}\geq\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}((1+t)/2)
. Now by Fact
11
and Condition
1
,
\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}((1+t)/2)\to_{p}G_{0}^{-1}((1+t)/2)
. However,
G_{0}^{-1}((1+t)/2)>G_{0}^{-1}(t)
, where the strict inequality follows because
g_{0}
being log-concave, is positive on
J(G_{0})
, indicating
G_{0}^{-1}
is strictly increasing on
(0,1)
. Thus it follows that
P(\xi_{n}\geq G_{0}^{-1}(t))\to 1
. Therefore the proof follows by contradiction.

Case (b)

Since
\xi_{n}\to_{p}\infty
does not hold, there exists
M>0
and a subsequence
n_{k}
so that
\liminf_{k}P(\xi_{n_{k}}<M)>0
. To avoid cumbersome notation, we will denote
\xi_{n_{k}}
by
\xi_{n}
from now on. Note that
\xi_{n}<M
implies
\tilde{G}_{n}(\xi_{n})\leq\tilde{G}_{n}(M)
. On one hand,
\tilde{G}_{n}(\xi_{n})=1-\eta_{n}
by Lemma A.3.5 of
Bobkov and Ledoux 2014
because
\tilde{G}_{n}
is continuous on
J(\tilde{G}_{n})
. On the other hand,
\tilde{G}_{n}(M)\to_{p}G_{0}(M)
by Condition
1
. Note that since
G_{0}^{-1}(1)=\infty
,
G_{0}(M)<1
. Specifically, there exists
z<1
so that
G_{0}(M)<z
. Therefore, we have shown that
P(1-\eta_{n}\leq z)\to_{n}1
.
However, the above can not hold since
\eta_{n}\to 0
. Thus, there is no
M
so that
\liminf_{n}P(\xi_{n}<M)>0
holds. We have come to a contradiction again, which concludes our proof.

∎

Lemma A.4
.

Suppose either
\tilde{g}_{n}\in\mathcal{SLC}_{0}
is a density satisfying Condition
1
and Condition
2
, or
\tilde{g}_{n}
satisfies Condition
3
. Let
\eta_{n}=Cn^{-2p/5}
where
p
is as in Condition
2
(or Condition
3
) and
C>0
. Then

\xi_{n}\leq\frac{-\log 2+2p(\log n)/5}{\omega_{n}}

where
\omega_{n}
is as in Fact
4
. In fact,

|\xi_{n}|=O_{p}(\log n)
.

Proof of Lemma
A.4
.

Observe that if
\tilde{g}_{n}\in\mathcal{SLC}_{0}
, then
\tilde{g}_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(z))>0
for
z\in(0,1)
. If
\tilde{g}_{n}
satisfies Condition
3
, then also the above holds because by Condition
3
,
\tilde{g}_{n}>0
on
\mathbb{R}
. Since
\tilde{g}_{n}
is symmetric about zero,
0=\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(1/2)
. Noting
\xi_{n}=-\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(\eta_{n})
, we therefore derive that

\displaystyle\xi_{n}=\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(1/2)-\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(\eta_{n})=\displaystyle\int_{\eta_{n}}^{1/2}\frac{dz}{\tilde{g}_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(z))}\leq\displaystyle\int_{\eta_{n}}^{1/2}\frac{dz}{\omega_{n}\tilde{G}_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(z))},

where
\omega_{n}
is as in Fact
4
.
Because
\tilde{g}_{n}>0
on
J(\tilde{G}_{n})
, it follows that
\tilde{G}_{n}
is continuous on
J(\tilde{G}_{n})
. Therefore, we have

\tilde{G}_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(z))=z
, implying

\xi_{n}\leq\frac{\log(1/2)-\log(\eta_n)}{\omega_{n}}=\frac{-\log 2+2p(\log n)/5}{\omega_{n}}.

Since
\omega_{n}\to_{p}\omega_{0}>0
by Fact
4
, the proof follows.
∎

Lemma A.5
.

Consider the set up of Lemma
A.4
. Let
\tilde{\xi}_{n}=\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(\eta_{n}/2)
.
Suppose
y_{n}
is a sequence of non-negative random variables so that
P(y_{n}<\eta_{n}/(2g_{0}(0)))\to 1
. Then

\displaystyle P([-\xi_{n}-y_{n},\xi_{n}+y_{n}]\subset[-\tilde{\xi}_{n},\tilde{\xi}_{n}])\to 1.

(41)

Proof of Lemma
A.5
.

Under our set up,
\tilde{g}_{n}
is positive on the set
J(\tilde{G}_{n})
. Therefore the function
\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}
is continuous on
(0,1)
. Hence the mean value theorem implies

\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(\eta_{n})-\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(\eta_{n}/2)=\frac{\eta_{n}}{2\tilde{g}_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(t))}\geq\frac{\eta_{n}}{2\|\tilde{g}_{n}\|_{\infty}}

for some
t\in[\eta_{n}/2,\eta_{n}]
.
Condition
1
implies that
\|\tilde{g}_{n}\|_{\infty}\to_{p}\|g_{0}\|_{\infty}=g_{0}(0)
.
Therefore, as
n\to\infty
,

P\bigg(\liminf_{n}\frac{\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(\eta_{n})-\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(\eta_{n}/2)}{\eta_{n}}\geq\frac{1}{2g_{0}(0)}\bigg)\to 1.

Hence if
y_{n}<\eta_{n}/(2g_{0}(0))
, then

\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(\eta_{n})-y_{n}\geq\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(\eta_{n}/2)
with probability tending to one.
Since
\tilde{g}_{n}
is symmetric about zero, we obtain that

\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(1-\eta_{n})+y_{n}<\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(1-\eta_{n}/2)

with probability tending to one.
Since
\xi_{n}=\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(1-\eta_{n})
and
\tilde{\xi}_{n}=\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(1-\eta_{n}/2)
, the proof follows.
∎

Lemma A.6
.

Consider the set up of Lemma
A.4
. Then for
y_{n}=o_{p}(\eta_{n})
, we have

P\Big([-\xi_{n}-|y_{n}|,\xi_{n}+|y_{n}|]\subset\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0}))\Big)\to 1.

Proof of Lemma
A.6
.

Letting
\tilde{\xi}_{n}=-\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(\eta_{n}/2)
, and applying Lemma
A.2
, we obtain
P([-\tilde{\xi}_{n},\tilde{\xi}_{n}]\subset\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0})))\to 1
. Then the result follows from Lemma
A.5
.
∎

A.2.2
lemmas on
\tilde{g}_{n}
and
g_{0}
:

Lemma A.7
.

Suppose
\xi_{n}=\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(1-\eta_{n})
where
\tilde{g}_{n}\in\mathcal{SLC}_{0}
satisfies Condition
1
. Then

A.

\sup_{x\in[-\xi_{n},\xi_{n}]}\tilde{g}_{n}(x)=O_{p}(1)
and
\sup_{x\in[-\xi_{n},\xi_{n}]}\tilde{g}_{n}(x)^{-1}=O_{p}(\eta^{-1}_{n})
.

B.

\tilde{\psi}_{n}=\log\tilde{g}_{n}
satisfies
\sup_{x\in[-\xi_{n},\xi_{n}]}\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x)=O_{p}(1)
. For
\eta_{n}=Cn^{-2p/5}
with
p\in(0,1)
and
C>0
, we have
\sup_{x\in[-\xi_{n},\xi_{n}]}(-\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x))=O_{p}(\log n)
.

Proof of Lemma
A.7
.

The upper bound on
\tilde{g}_{n}
follows from Fact
1
and Condition
1
. For the upper bound on
\tilde{g}_{n}^{-1}
, note that Fact
4
implies that

\displaystyle\tilde{g}_{n}(x)\geq

\displaystyle\ w_{n}\min(\tilde{G}_{n}(x),1-\tilde{G}_{n}(x)),\quad\text{for all }x\in\mathbb{R}.

Since
\tilde{G}_{n}
is a non-decreasing and
1-\tilde{G}_{n}
is a non-increasing function, any
x\in[-\xi_{n},\xi_{n}]
satisfies

\tilde{g}_{n}(x)\geq{\omega_{n}}{\min(\tilde{G}_{n}(-\xi_{n}),1-\tilde{G}_{n}(\xi_{n}))}=\omega_{n}\eta_{n}

because
\tilde{G}_{n}(-\xi_{n})=\eta_{n}
.
Since the random variable
\omega_{n}\to_{p}\omega_{0}>0
by Fact
4
,
part A of the current lemma follows.
Part B follows directly from Part A.
∎

Lemma A.8
.

Assume
f_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}
. Suppose
\{\tilde{g}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
is a sequence of log-concave densities satisfying
\|\tilde{g}_{n}-g_{0}\|_{\infty}\to_{n}0
. Then the following hold for any
y_{n}\to_{n}0
:

(A)

Let
\tilde{\psi}_{n}=\log\tilde{g}_{n}
. Then

\tilde{\psi}_{n}(\mathord{\cdot}+y_{n})\to_{n}\psi_{0}
everywhere on
\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0}))
.

(B)

\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{\prime}(\mathord{\cdot}+y_{n})\to_{n}\psi_{0}^{\prime}
Lebesgue almost everywhere on
\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0}))
. In particular, if
x
is a continuity point of
\psi_{0}^{\prime}
, then
\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{\prime}(x+y_{n})\to_{n}\psi_{0}^{\prime}
.

Proof of Lemma
A.8
.

By our assumptions on
g_{0}
,
\mathcal{I}_{g_{0}}<\infty
. Therefore,
g_{0}
is absolutely continuous
(
Huber 1964
, Theorem 3,)
.
Hence,
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}|\tilde{g}_{n}(x+y_{n})-g_{0}(x)|\to_{n}0
. Since for each
x\in\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0}))
, there exists an open neighborhood around
x
where
|\psi_{0}|<\infty
,
\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x+y_{n})\to_{n}\psi_{0}(x)
for each
x\in\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0}))
. Therefore part (A) follows. For part (B), first note that if
x\in\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0}))
is a continuity point of
\psi_{0}^{\prime}
, then
\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(x+y_{n})\to_{n}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(x)
by Theorem 25.7 of
Rockafellar 1970
. Now since
\psi_{0}
is concave,
\psi_{0}
is continuously differentiable at
x
if it is differentiable at
x

(
Rockafellar 1970
, Corollary 25.5.1)
.
However, a concave
\psi_{0}
is differentiable Lebesgue almost everywhere on
\mathrm{dom}(\phi_{0})

(
Rockafellar 1970
, Theorem 25.5)
. Therefore, the lemma follows.
∎

A.2.3
Lemmas on
g_{0}
:

Lemma A.9
.

Suppose
g_{0}
satisfies Assumption
A
. Let
\kappa
be as in Assumption
A
. Then for any
x\in\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0})
,
\psi_{0}
satisfies

|\psi_{0}(x)|\leq|\psi_{0}(0)|+\kappa x^{2}.

In particular, if
\eta_{n}=Cn^{-p}
for
p\in(0,1)
and
C>0
, then under the set up of Lemma
A.4
,
\xi_{n}=\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(1-\eta_{n})
satisfies

\sup_{x\in[-\xi_{n},\xi_{n}]}|\psi_{0}(x)|=O_{p}((\log n)^{2}).

Proof of Lemma
A.9
.

Because
\psi_{0}\in\mathcal{SC}_{0}
, zero is the mode of
\psi_{0}
. Therefore, the upper bound on
\psi_{0}
follows since
\psi_{0}(x)<\psi_{0}(0)
.
For the lower bound, first note that the concavity of
\psi_{0}
indicates that if
x\geq 0
and
x\in\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0})
, then

\psi_{0}(x)\geq\psi_{0}(0)+\psi^{\prime}_{0}(x-)x.

By our notation,
\psi_{0}^{\prime}(x+)=\psi_{0}^{\prime}(x)
.
Noting Assumption
A
implies
\psi_{0}^{\prime}(x-)\geq\psi_{0}^{\prime}(0-)-\kappa x
, we derive

\psi_{0}(x)\geq\psi_{0}(0)+\psi_{0}^{\prime}(0-)x-\kappa x^{2}.

Since
\psi_{0}^{\prime}(0-)\geq 0
, the above yields
\psi_{0}(x)\geq\psi_{0}(0)-\kappa x^{2}
for all
x\geq 0
.
Since
\psi_{0}
is symmetric about zero, we derive that
\psi_{0}(x)\geq\psi_{0}(0)-\kappa x^{2}
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
. In conjunction with the fact that
\psi_{0}(x)\leq\psi_{0}(0)
, the latter implies
|\psi_{0}(x)|\leq|\psi_{0}(0)|+\kappa x^{2}
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
. Since
P([-\xi_{n},\xi_{n}]\subset\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0}))\to 1
by Lemma
A.2
, the rest of the proof follows noting

\xi_{n}=O_{p}(\log n)
for
\eta_{n}=n^{-2p/5}
by Lemma
A.4
.
∎

Lemma A.10
.

Suppose
\psi_{0}\in\mathcal{SC}_{0}
satisfies Assumption
A
. Further suppose
\eta_{n}
is as in Lemma
A.9
and
y_{n}>0
satisfies
y_{n}=o_{p}(\eta_{n})
. Then

\sup_{x\in[-\xi_{n}-y_{n},\xi_{n}+y_{n}]}|\psi_{0}^{\prime}(x)|=O_{p}(-\log(\eta_n)).

Proof of Lemma
A.10
.

Since
\psi_{0}\in\mathcal{SC}_{0}
,
\psi_{0}^{\prime}
attains its maxima on any interval at the endpoints.
Lemma
A.6
implies
[-\xi_{n}-y_{n},\xi_{n}+y_{n}]\subset\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0})
with probability approaching one. Therefore
Assumption
A
implies

\psi_{0}^{\prime}(-\xi_{n}-y_{n})\leq|\psi_{0}^{\prime}(0)|+\kappa(|\xi_{n}|+y_{n}).

(42)

Rest of the proof follows from Lemma
A.4
and the fact that
y_{n}=o_{p}(1)
.
∎

Lemma A.11
.

Under the set up of Theorem
1
, there exists
C>0
so that if
b>0
satisfies
[-b-|\tilde{\delta}_{n}|,b+|\tilde{\delta}_{n}|]\subset\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0}))
, then

\sup_{t\in[-b,b]}\frac{g_{0}(t)}{g_{0}(t+\tilde{\delta}_{n})}\leq e^{|\tilde{\delta}_{n}|(C+\kappa b+\kappa|\tilde{\delta}_{n}|)}.

Proof of Lemma
A.11
.

Recalling that we use
\psi_{0}^{\prime}
to denote the right derivative of
\psi_{0}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{g_{0}(t)}{g_{0}(t+\tilde{\delta}_{n})}=

\displaystyle\ \exp(\psi_0(t)-\psi_0(t+\td))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\ \exp(|\td|\max\{|\psi_0'(t)|,|\psi_0'(t+\td)|\})

where (a) follows from (
35
).
If
t,\ t-\tilde{\delta}_{n}\in\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0}))
, by
Assumption
A
, it also holds that
|\psi_{0}^{\prime}(t)|\leq\psi_{0}^{\prime}(0-)+\kappa|t|
and
|\psi_{0}^{\prime}(t+\tilde{\delta}_{n})|\leq\psi_{0}^{\prime}(0-)+\kappa|t|+\kappa|\tilde{\delta}_{n}|
. Thus for
C=\psi^{\prime}_{0}(0-)
, we obtain that

\frac{g_{0}(t)}{g_{0}(t+\tilde{\delta}_{n})}\leq e^{|\tilde{\delta}_{n}|(C+\kappa|t|+\kappa|\tilde{\delta}_{n}|)},

from which, the result follows.

∎

A.2.4
Lemmas on
\tilde{\psi}_{n}
:

Lemma A.12
.

Suppose
\tilde{g}_{n}
satisfies Condition
1
and Condition
2
with
p\in(0,1)
. Further suppose
a_{n}
and
\tilde{g}_{n}
satisfies

\sup_{x\in[-a_{n},a_{n}]}(|\psi_{0}(x)|+|\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x)|)=O_{p}((\log n)^{2}).

(43)

Then

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x)-\psi_{0}(x))^{2}g_{0}(x)dx=

\displaystyle O_{p}((\log n)^{4}n^{-2p}),

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x)-\psi_{0}(x))^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(x)dx=

\displaystyle O_{p}((\log n)^{4}n^{-2p}).

In particular, if
\tilde{g}_{n}\in\mathcal{SLC}_{0}
, then (
43
) holds with
a_{n}=\xi_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n})=\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(1-\eta_{n})
, where
\eta_{n}=Cn^{-2p/5}
for some
C>0
.

Proof of Lemma
A.12
.

We first invoke an algebraic fact. For any
x,y>0
,

(\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{y})^{2}=\min(x,y)\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{\max(x,y)}{\min(x,y)}}-1\bigg)^{2}=\min(x,y)\Big(e^{|\log x-\log y|/2}-1\Big)^{2}.

Since for any
z>0
,
z
and
z^{2}/2
are bounded above by
e^{z}-1
, it follows that

\Big(e^{|\log x-\log y|/2}-1\Big)^{2}\geq(\log x-\log y)^{2}/4,(\log x-\log y)^{4}/8^{2}.

Thus

\displaystyle 4\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\sqrt{\tilde{g}_{n}(x)}-\sqrt{g_{0}(x)})^{2}dx\geq

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\min(\tilde{g}_{n}(x),g_{0}(x))(\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x)-\psi_{0}(x))^{2}dx

\displaystyle 8^{2}\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\sqrt{\tilde{g}_{n}(x)}-\sqrt{g_{0}(x)})^{2}dx\geq

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\min(\tilde{g}_{n}(x),g_{0}(x))(\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x)-\psi_{0}(x))^{4}dx.

(44)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x)-\psi_{0}(x))^{2}g_{0}(x)dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x)-\psi_{0}(x))^{2}\Big(g_{0}(x)-\min(g_{0}(x),\tilde{g}_{n}(x))\Big)dx

\displaystyle\ +\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x)-\psi_{0}(x))^{2}\min(g_{0}(x),\tilde{g}_{n}(x))dx

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x)-\psi_{0}(x))^{2}(g_{0}(x)-\tilde{g}_{n}(x))1_{[\tilde{g}_{n}<g_{0}]}dx+O_{p}(n^{-2p})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \underbrace{\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x)-\psi_{0}(x))^{2}(\sqrt{g_{0}(x)}-\sqrt{\tilde{g}_{n}(x)})^{2}1_{[\tilde{g}_{n}<g_{0}]}dx}_{T_{1}}

\displaystyle-2

\displaystyle\ \underbrace{\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x)-\psi_{0}(x))^{2}\sqrt{\tilde{g}_{n}(x)}(\sqrt{\tilde{g}_{n}(x)}-\sqrt{g_{0}(x)})1_{[\tilde{g}_{n}<g_{0}]}dx}_{T_{2}}+O_{p}(n^{-2p})

where (a) follows from (
A.2.4
) and Condition
2
.
We can upper bound
|\psi_{0}(x)-\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x)|
noting

\sup_{x\in[-a_{n},a_{n}]}|\psi_{0}(x)-\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x)|\leq\sup_{x\in[-a_{n},a_{n}]}(|\psi_{0}(x)|+|\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x)|)=O_{p}((\log n)^{2})

by (
43
).
Therefore
T_{1}\leq O_{p}((\log n)^{4})H(\tilde{g}_{n},g_{0})^{2}
, which is
O_{p}((\log n)^{4}n^{-2p})
. On the other hand, noting
T_{2}
can be written as

\displaystyle T_{2}=

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x)-\psi_{0}(x))^{2}\sqrt{\min(\tilde{g}_{n}(x),g_{0}(x))}(\sqrt{\tilde{g}_{n}(x)}-\sqrt{g_{0}(x)})1_{[\tilde{g}_{n}<g_{0}]}dx,

by an application of the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we derive

|T_{2}|\leq\bigg(\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x)-\psi_{0}(x))^{4}\min(\tilde{g}_{n}(x),g_{0}(x))dx\bigg)^{1/2}H(\tilde{g}_{n},g_{0}),

which, by (
A.2.4
) and Condition
2
, is
O_{p}(n^{-2p})
, thus completing the proof of the first part.

It remains to show that (
43
) holds when
\tilde{g}_{n}\in\mathcal{SLC}_{0}
and
a_{n}=\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(1-\eta_{n})
.
Lemma
A.9
entails that this
a_{n}
satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[-a_{n},a_{n}]}|\psi_{0}(x)|=O_{p}((\log n)^{2}).

(45)

The proof of the current lemma then follows noting Lemma
A.7
implies

\sup_{x\in[-\xi_{n},\xi_{n}]}|\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x)|=O_{p}(\log n).

∎

A.2.5
Lemmas on
\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{\prime}
:

Lemma A.13
.

Let
\rho_{n}=\eta_{n}/\log n
. Suppose
\tilde{g}_{n}
is a log-concave density satisfying Condition
1
and Condition
2
. Let
a_{n}
be a positive sequence satisfying (
43
) such that
a_{n}=O_{p}(\log n)
,

P\Big([-a_{n}-\rho_{n},a_{n}+\rho_{n}]\subset\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0}))\cap\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}))\Big)\to 1,

(46)

\displaystyle P(\tilde{G}_{n}(-a_{n})>\eta_{n}/4,1-\tilde{G}_{n}(a_{n})>\eta_{n}/4)\to 1.

(47)

Then

\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\Big(\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)\Big)^{2}dz=O_{p}((\log n)^{6}n^{-4p/5}),

\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\Big(\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(z)-\psi^{\prime}_{0}(z)\Big)^{2}\mu_{n}(z)dz=O_{p}((\log n)^{6}n^{-4p/5})),

for any density
\mu_{n}
such that
\|\mu_{n}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(1)
, where
p
is as in Condition
2
. In particular, the lemma holds if

\tilde{g}_{n}\in\mathcal{SLC}_{0}
and
a_{n}=\xi_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n})=\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(1-\eta_{n})
, where
\eta_{n}=Cn^{-2p/5}
for some
C>0
.

Proof of Lemma
A.13
.

Since
\tilde{\psi}_{n}
is concave and
\rho_{n}>0
, any
z\in\mathrm{dom}(\tilde{\psi}_{n})
satisfies

\frac{\tilde{\psi}_{n}(z+\rho_{n})-\tilde{\psi}_{n}(z)}{\rho_{n}}\leq\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(z+)\leq\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(z-)\leq\frac{\tilde{\psi}_{n}(z)-\tilde{\psi}_{n}(z-\rho_{n})}{\rho_{n}}.

(48)

Now suppose (
46
) holds.
Then
the quantities

\Delta_{n}^{+}(z)=\frac{\tilde{\psi}_{n}(z+\rho_{n})-\tilde{\psi}_{n}(z)}{\rho_{n}}-\frac{\psi_{0}(z+\rho_{n})-\psi_{0}(z)}{\rho_{n}},

and

\Delta_{n}^{-}(z)=\frac{\tilde{\psi}_{n}(z)-\tilde{\psi}_{n}(z-\rho_{n})}{\rho_{n}}-\frac{\psi_{0}(z)-\psi_{0}(z-\rho_{n})}{\rho_{n}}

are well defined for all
z\in[-a_{n},a_{n}]
.
Recalling
\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{\prime}(z)=\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{\prime}(z+)
and
\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)=\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z+)

by our notation, we can then show that under (
46
),

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{\prime}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\ \frac{\tilde{\psi}_{n}(z)-\tilde{\psi}_{n}(z-\rho_{n})}{\rho_{n}}-\frac{\psi_{0}(z)-\psi_{0}(z-\rho_{n})}{\rho_{n}}

\displaystyle\ +\bigg(\frac{\psi_{0}(z)-\psi_{0}(z-\rho_{n})}{\rho_{n}}-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)\bigg)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\Delta_{n}^{-}(z)+\kappa\rho_{n}

for all
z\in[-a_{n},a_{n}]
, where (a) follows by (
48
), and (b) follows because

\frac{\psi_{0}(z)-\psi_{0}(z-\rho_{n})}{\rho_{n}}-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)\leq\rho_{n}^{-1}\bigg|\displaystyle\int_{z-\rho_{n}}^{z}\Big(\psi_{0}^{\prime}(t)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)\Big)dt\bigg|\leq\kappa\rho_{n}/2

since Assumption
A
applies on the set
[z-\rho_{n},z]\subset\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0}))
.
Similarly, we can show that

\Delta_{n}^{+}(z)-\kappa\rho_{n}\leq\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{\prime}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)\quad\text{for all }z\in[-a_{n},a_{n}],

provided (
46
) holds. Thus we have established that

|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)|\leq\max\{\Delta_{n}^{+}(z),\Delta_{n}^{-}(z)\}+\kappa\rho_{n}

(49)

whenever (
46
) holds.
Now observe that the integral
\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\Delta^{+}_{n}(z)^{2}dz
is well defined under (
46
), and equals

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\bigg(\frac{\tilde{\psi}_{n}(z+\rho_{n})-\tilde{\psi}_{n}(z)}{\rho_{n}}-\frac{\psi_{0}(z+\rho_{n})-\psi_{0}(z)}{\rho_{n}}\bigg)^{2}dz

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ 2\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\bigg(\frac{\tilde{\psi}_{n}(z+\rho_{n})-\psi_{0}(z+\rho_{n})}{\rho_{n}}\bigg)^{2}dz+2\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\bigg(\frac{\tilde{\psi}_{n}(z)-\psi_{0}(z)}{\rho_{n}}\bigg)^{2}dz

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\ \frac{2}{\rho_{n}^{2}\min(\tilde{g}_{n}(a_{n}+\rho_{n}),\tilde{g}_{n}(-a_{n}+\rho_{n}))}\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}(z+\rho_{n})-\psi_{0}(z+\rho_{n}))^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(z+\rho_{n})dz

\displaystyle\ +\frac{2}{\rho_{n}^{2}\min(\tilde{g}_{n}(a_{n}),\tilde{g}_{n}(-a_{n}))}\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}(z)-\psi_{0}(z))^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(z)dz

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \frac{O_{p}((\log n)^{4}n^{-2p})}{\rho^{2}_{n}\min(\tilde{g}_{n}(a_{n}+\rho_{n}),\tilde{g}_{n}(-a_{n}-\rho_{n}))},

where (a) follows because
\tilde{g}_{n}
being log-concave, and hence unimodal, attains minimum over an interval at either of the endpoints; and (b) follows from Lemma
A.12
and the fact that
a_{n}
and
\rho_{n}
are positive.
Let us define

\displaystyle\epsilon_{n}(\rho_{n})=\frac{(\log n)^{4}n^{-2p}}{\rho^{2}_{n}\min(\tilde{g}_{n}(a_{n}+\rho_{n}),\tilde{g}_{n}(-a_{n}-\rho_{n}))}.

(50)

Since (
46
) holds with probability tending to one by our assumption, we can write

\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\Delta^{+}_{n}(z)^{2}dz=O_{p}(\epsilon_{n}(\rho_{n}))
.
Similarly, we can show that

\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\Delta^{-}_{n}(z)dz
is
O_{p}(\epsilon_{n}(\rho_{n}))
.
The above, combined with (
49
), leads to

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z))^{2}dz\leq

\displaystyle\ 2\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\Delta_{n}^{-}(z)^{2}dz+2\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\Delta_{n}^{+}(z)^{2}dz+4\kappa^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}a_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ O_{p}(\epsilon_{n}(\rho_{n}))+O_{p}(\rho_{n}^{2}a_{n}).

Note that
\rho_{n}^{2}a_{n}=O(n^{-4p/5}/\log n)
because
\rho_{n}=\eta_{n}/\log n
and
a_{n}=O_{p}(\log n)
by our assumption. Also,
\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z))^{2}\mu_{n}(z)dz
can be bounded by

\displaystyle 2\|f\|_{\infty}\bigg(\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\Delta_{n}^{-}(z)^{2}dz+\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\Delta_{n}^{+}(z)^{2}dz\bigg)+2\kappa^{2}\rho^{2}_{n}

which is
O_{p}(\epsilon_{n}(\rho_{n}))+O_{p}(n^{-4/5}/(\log n)^{2})
because
\|\mu_{n}\|_{\infty}
is
O_{p}(1)
and
\rho_{n}
equals
\eta_{n}/\log n
.
To prove the first part of the lemma, it only remains to show that

\epsilon_{n}(\rho_{n})=O_{p}((\log n)^{6}n^{-4p/5}).

(51)

Since
a_{n}-\rho_{n}\in\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}))
under (
46
),
Fact
4
implies

\tilde{g}_{n}(-a_{n}-\rho_{n})\geq\omega_{n}\tilde{G}_{n}(-a_{n}-\rho_{n})=\omega_{n}\tilde{G}_{n}(-a_{n})-\omega_{n}\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}-\rho_{n}}^{-a_{n}}\tilde{g}_{n}(z)dz

under (
46
),
where
\omega_{n}
is as in fact
4
.
Note that

\bigg|\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}-\rho_{n}}^{-a_{n}}\tilde{g}_{n}(z)dz\bigg|\leq\rho_{n}\|\tilde{g}_{n}\|_{\infty}.

Also since
P(\tilde{G}_{n}(-a_{n})>\eta_{n}/4)\to 1
by our assumption, the following hold with probability tending to one,

\tilde{g}_{n}(-a_{n}-\rho_{n})\geq\omega_{n}(\eta_{n}/4-\rho_{n}\|\tilde{g}_{n}\|_{\infty})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\geq}}\omega_{n}\eta_{n}/8,

where (a) follows because
\rho_{n}=o(\eta_{n})
and
\|\tilde{g}_{n}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(1)
by Condition
1
and Fact
1
.
However, since
\omega_{n}\to_{p}\omega_{0}
by Fact
4
, the last display implies

\tilde{g}_{n}(-a_{n}-\rho_{n})^{-1}
is
O_{p}(1/\eta_{n})
.
Similarly, we can show that
\tilde{g}_{n}(a_{n}+\rho_{n})^{-1}
is
O_{p}(1/\eta_{n})
.
Thus

\epsilon_{n}(\rho_{n})=\frac{O_{p}((\log n)^{4}n^{-2p})}{\eta_{n}\rho_{n}^{2}}

follows.
Since
\eta_{n}=n^{-2p/5}
and
\rho_{n}=\eta_{n}/\log n
, (
51
) follows, thus completing the proof of the first part of Lemma
A.13
.

Now suppose
\tilde{g}_{n}\in\mathcal{SLC}_{0}
and
a_{n}=\xi_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n})=\tilde{G}_{n}^{-1}(1-\eta_{n})
. They satisfy (
43
) by Lemma
A.12
. Also
\xi_{n}=O_{p}(\log n)
by Lemma
A.4
. Noting
\rho_{n}=o(\eta_{n})
, (
46
) follows from Lemma
A.5

and Lemma
A.6
.
Since (
47
) trivially holds, second part of Lemma
A.13
also follows.
∎

Lemma A.14
.

Suppose
\tilde{g}_{n}
satisfies Condition
1
and Condition
2
. Let
a_{n}
be a sequence of positive random variables satisfying

a_{n}=O_{p}(\log n),\quad P\Big(a_{n}\in\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0}))\Big)\to 1,\quad\text{and}\quad G_{0}(a_{n})\to_{p}1.

(52)

Further suppose that
\tilde{g}_{n}
and
a_{n}
satisfy

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z))^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(z)dz=O_{p}((\log n)^{6}n^{-4p/5}),

(53)

where
p
is as in Condition
2
.
Then

\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(z)^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(z)dz\to_{p}\mathcal{I}_{f_{0}}
. In addition, if
\tilde{g}_{n}\in\mathcal{SLC}_{0}
, then
a_{n}=\xi_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n})=(\tilde{G}_{n})^{-1}(1-\eta_{n})
where
\eta_{n}=Cn^{-2p/5}
for some
C>0
.

Proof of Lemma
A.14
.

Note that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\Big(\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(z)^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)^{2}g_{0}(z)\Big)dz

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\Big((\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z))^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(z)+2\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(z)\tilde{g}_{n}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)^{2}g_{0}(z)\Big)dz

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \underbrace{\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z))^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(z)dz}_{T_{1}}+2\underbrace{\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)(\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z))\tilde{g}_{n}(z)dz}_{T_{2}}

\displaystyle\ +\underbrace{\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)^{2}(\tilde{g}_{n}(z)-g_{0}(z))dz}_{T_{3}},

It is clear that by our assumption,

T_{1}=O_{p}((\log n)^{6}n^{-4p/5})
, which is
o_{p}(1)
.

Because
\psi_{0}^{\prime}
is a non-increasing odd function, on any interval,
|\psi_{0}^{\prime}|
attains its maximum at both end points. Therefore,

\displaystyle|T_{2}|\leq

\displaystyle\ |\psi_{0}^{\prime}(a_{n})|\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)|\tilde{g}_{n}(z)dz

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\ |\psi_{0}^{\prime}(a_{n})|\bigg(\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}(\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z))^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(z)dz\bigg)^{1/2},

where (a) follows by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Thus,
|T_{2}|\leq|\psi_{0}^{\prime}(a_{n})|\sqrt{T_{1}}
.
However, Assumption
A
implies that
|\psi_{0}^{\prime}(a_{n})|\leq|\psi_{0}^{\prime}(0)|+\kappa a_{n}=O_{p}(\log n)
provided
a_{n}\in\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0}))
. By our assumption on
a_{n}
, the latter holds with probability tending to one. Hence,

T_{2}=O_{p}(\log n)\sqrt{T_{1}}=O_{p}((\log n)^{4}n^{-2p/5})=o_{p}(1).

Finally, using the fact
|\psi_{0}^{\prime}(a_{n})|=O_{p}(\log n)
again, we bound
|T_{3}|
by

\displaystyle O_{p}((\log n)^{2})d_{TV}(\tilde{g}_{n},g_{0})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle O_{p}((\log n)^{2})H(\tilde{g}_{n},g_{0})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}O_{p}((\log n)^{2}n^{-p})=o_{p}(1),

where (a) follows from Fact
5
and (b) follows noting
H(\tilde{g}_{n},g_{0})=O_{p}(n^{-p})
by Condition
2
. Thus we have shown that

\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\Big(\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(z)^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)^{2}g_{0}(z)\Big)dz=o_{p}(1).

Since
a_{n}\to_{p}G_{0}^{-1}(1)
by our assumption, noting
G_{0}^{-1}(0)=-G_{0}^{-1}(1)
, we also obtain
-a_{n}\to_{p}G_{0}^{-1}(0)
. Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{-a_{n}}^{a_{n}}\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)^{2}g_{0}(z)dz\to_{p}\displaystyle\int_{G_{0}^{-1}(0)}^{G_{0}^{-1}(1)}\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)^{2}g_{0}(z)dz=\mathcal{I}_{f_{0}},

which completes the proof of the first part of Lemma
A.14
.
Second part of Lemma
A.14
follows noting
\xi_{n}=O_{p}(\log n)
by Lemma
A.4
,
\xi_{n}\in\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\psi_{0}))
with probability tending to one by Lemma
A.2
,
\xi_{n}\to_{p}G_{0}^{-1}(1)
by Lemma
A.3
, and
\tilde{g}_{n}\in\mathcal{SLC}_{0}
satisfies (
53
) by Lemma
A.13
.
∎

Lemma A.15
.

Consider the set up of Theorem
1
.
Suppose
\eta_{n}=Cn^{-2p/5}
, where
C>0
and
p
is as in Condition
2
. Let
y_{n}
be a sequence of positive random variables such that

P(|y_{n}|\leq\eta_{n}/(2g_{0}(0)))\to 1
. Then

\sup_{x\in[-\xi_{n}-y_{n},\xi_{n}+y_{n}]}|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(x)|=O_{p}(\eta_{n}^{-1/2})=O_{p}(n^{p/5}).

Proof of Lemma
A.15
.

Let
q\in(0,1/2)
.
Since
\tilde{g}_{n}
, being log-concave, is positive on
\mathrm{int}(J(\tilde{G}_{n}))
, using Fact
8
we obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(q/2)}^{\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(q)}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(x)^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(x)dx=\displaystyle\int_{q/2}^{q}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(z))^{2}dz.

Note that
\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}
is non-increasing and positive on
(-\infty,-x]
, and
\tilde{g}_{n}(-x)
is positive and non-decreasing on
(-\infty,-x]
. Thus
\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}\circ\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}
is non-increasing. Therefore

q\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(q))^{2}/2\leq\displaystyle\int_{\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(q/2)}^{\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(q)}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(x)^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(x)dx\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\displaystyle\int_{\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(q/2)}^{\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(1-q/2)}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(x)^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(x)dx

where (a) follows because
q<1-q/2
for all
q\in(0,1/2)
.
Suppose
q=\eta_{n}/2
. Note that
\tilde{\xi}_{n}=-\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(\eta_{n}/2)
and
|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}|
is symmetric about zero. Then the last display leads to

\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(\tilde{\xi}_{n})^{2}\leq\frac{4\displaystyle\int_{\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(\eta_{n}/4)}^{\tilde{G}^{-1}_{n}(1-\eta_{n}/4)}\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(x)^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(x)dx}{\eta_{n}}.

From Lemma
A.14
it follows that the integral converges in probability to
\mathcal{I}_{f_{0}}
. Therefore,
|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(\tilde{\xi}_{n})|=O_{p}(\eta_{n}^{-1/2})
which implies

\sup_{x\in[-\tilde{\xi}_{n},\tilde{\xi}_{n}]}|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(x)|=O_{p}(\eta_{n}^{-1/2}).

(54)

The rest of the proof follows from (
54
) and Lemma
A.5
.

∎

Lemma A.16
.

Consider the set up of Theorem
1
.
Then
\|h_{n}\|_{P_{0},2}^{2}=O_{p}(n^{-4p/5}(\log n)^{3})
, where
h_{n}
is as defined in (
21
).

Proof of Lemma
A.16
.

Note that
\|h_{n}\|_{P_{0},2}^{2}
equals

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{-\xi_{n}}^{\xi_{n}}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{\prime}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z-\tilde{\delta}_{n}))^{2}f_{0}(z+\bar{\theta}_{n})dz

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ 2\displaystyle\int_{-\xi_{n}}^{\xi_{n}}\Big(\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{\prime}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)\Big)^{2}f_{0}(z+\bar{\theta}_{n})dz+2\displaystyle\int_{-\xi_{n}}^{\xi_{n}}\Big(\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z-\tilde{\delta}_{n})-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)\Big)^{2}f_{0}(z+\bar{\theta}_{n})dz

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ 2\underbrace{\displaystyle\int_{-\xi_{n}}^{\xi_{n}}\Big(\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{\prime}(z)-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)\Big)^{2}g_{0}(z-\tilde{\delta}_{n})dz}_{T_{1}}+2\underbrace{\displaystyle\int_{-\xi_{n}}^{\xi_{n}}\Big(\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z-\tilde{\delta}_{n})-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)\Big)^{2}g_{0}(z-\tilde{\delta}_{n})dz}_{T_{2}}.

T_{1}
is
O_{p}(n^{-4p/5}(\log n)^{6})
by Lemma
A.13
. Since Assumption
A
implies
\psi_{0}^{\prime}
is Lipschitz with constant
\kappa
on its domain, Lemma
A.6
entails that
T_{2}
is bounded by

2\kappa^{2}\tilde{\delta}_{n}^{2}\displaystyle\int_{-\xi_{n}}^{\xi_{n}}g_{0}(z-\tilde{\delta}_{n})dz

which is
O_{p}(n^{-1})
since
\tilde{\delta}_{n}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
. Hence, the proof follows.
∎

A.2.6
Lemma on consistency of Fisher information
\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})
:

Lemma A.17
.

Under the set up of Theorem
1
,
\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})\to_{p}\mathcal{I}_{f_{0}}
where
\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})
is as defined in (
8
).

of Lemma
A.17
.

Denoting
\tilde{\delta}_{n}=\theta_{0}-\bar{\theta}_{n}
, we observe that

\displaystyle|\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})-\tilde{\mathcal{I}}_{f_{0}}(\eta_{n})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \underbrace{\bigg|\displaystyle\int_{\bar{\theta}_{n}-\xi_{n}}^{\bar{\theta}_{n}+\xi_{n}}\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{\prime}(x-\bar{\theta}_{n})^{2}d(\mathbb{F}_{n}-F_{0})(x)\bigg|}_{T_{1}}+\underbrace{\bigg|\displaystyle\int_{-\xi_{n}}^{\xi_{n}}\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{\prime}(x)^{2}\Big(g_{0}(x-\tilde{\delta}_{n})-g_{0}(x)\Big)dx\bigg|}_{T_{2}}

\displaystyle\ +\underbrace{\bigg|\displaystyle\int_{-\xi_{n}}^{\xi_{n}}\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{\prime}(x)^{2}\Big(g_{0}(x)-\tilde{g}_{n}(x)\Big)dx\bigg|}_{T_{3}}+\underbrace{\bigg|\displaystyle\int_{-\xi_{n}}^{\xi_{n}}\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{\prime}(x)^{2}\tilde{g}_{n}(x)dx-\mathcal{I}_{f_{0}}\bigg|}_{T_{4}}

(55)

Let us consider the term
T_{1}
first. Denoting
M_{n}=Cn^{p/5}
as in the
the proof of the first step of Theorem
1
, we recall the class of functions
\mathcal{U}_{n}(M_{n})
defined in (
24
).

In the same way we showed that
h_{n}\in\mathcal{H}_{n}(C)
with high probability in the proof of the first step of Theorem
1
,
we can show that the function defined by

\tilde{h}_{n}(x)=\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}(x-\bar{\theta}_{n})^{2}1_{[\bar{\theta}_{n}-\xi_{n},\bar{\theta}_{n}+\xi_{n}]}(x)

is a member of the class

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{n}(C)=\bigg\{h:\mathbb{R}\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}\ \bigg|\ h(x)=u(x)^{2}1_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}(x),\ u\in\mathcal{U}_{n}(M_{n}),

\displaystyle\ [r_{1},r_{2}]\subset[\theta_{0}-C\log n,\theta_{0}+C\log n]\cap\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\phi_{0}))\bigg\}

with high probability for all large
n
provided
C>0
is sufficiently large. Using (
26
), (
27
) and following some standard calculations,
we can show that

\sup_{Q}\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,\mathcal{V}_{n}(C),L_{2}(Q)\lesssim M_{n}^{2}\epsilon^{-1},

where the supremum is over all probability measures on
\mathbb{R}
.
Because bracketing number is larger than covering number, it also follows that

\sup_{Q}\log N(\epsilon,\mathcal{V}_{n}(C),L_{2}(Q))\lesssim M_{n}^{2}\epsilon^{-1}.

The definition of
\mathcal{U}_{n}(M_{n})
in (
24
) implies that the functions in
\mathcal{V}_{n}(C)
are uniformly bounded by
M_{n}^{2}
.
Since for any fixed
\epsilon>0
,

M_{n}^{2}\sup_{Q}\log N(\epsilon,\mathcal{V}_{n}(C),L_{2}(Q))\lesssim M_{n}^{4}\epsilon^{-1}=O(n^{-4p/5}),

Fact
14
leads to
E\|\mathbb{F}_{n}-F_{0}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{n}(C)}=o(1)
. Thus Markov’s inequality yields that
\|\mathbb{F}_{n}-F_{0}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{n}(C)}=o_{p}(1)
. Since for large
C
,
P(\tilde{h}_{n}\in\mathcal{V}_{n}(C))
with high probability, it can be shown that
\int\tilde{h}_{n}d(\mathbb{F}_{n}-F_{0})\to_{p}0
, which establishes
T_{1}=o_{p}(1)
.

Since the supremum of
|\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{n}|
over
[-\xi_{n},\xi_{n}]
is
O_{p}(n^{p/5})
by Lemma
A.15
,
we obtain that

T_{2}\leq O_{p}(n^{2p/5})d_{TV}(g_{0}(\cdot-\tilde{\delta}_{n}),g_{0})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}O_{p}(n^{2p/5})H(g_{0}(\cdot-\tilde{\delta}_{n}),g_{0})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}O_{p}(\tilde{\delta}_{n}n^{2p/5}),

which is
o_{p}(1)
because
\tilde{\delta}_{n}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
and
p\in(0,1)
. Here (a) and (b) follow from Fact
5
and Fact
15
, respectively.
In a similar way we can show that

T_{3}\leq O_{p}(n^{2p/5})d_{TV}(\tilde{g}_{n},g_{0})\leq O_{p}(n^{2p/5})H(\tilde{g}_{n},g_{0}),

which is
O_{p}(n^{-3p/5})
by Condition
2
.
Finally, noting
T_{4}
is also
o_{p}(1)
by Lemma
A.14
, the proof follows from (
A.2.6
).
∎

B
Proof of proposition
1

We will first show that
\widehat{g}_{\bar{\theta}_{n}}
and
\tilde{g}_{n}^{geo,sym}
satisfy Condition
1
. Then using this result, we will show in Lemma
B.2
and Lemma
B.3
that
\widehat{g}_{\bar{\theta}_{n}}
and
\tilde{g}_{n}^{geo,sym}
satisfy Condition
2
, respectively. To show that Condition
1
holds for these two densities, we prove a general Proposition which states that Condition
1
holds for all the density estimators of
g_{0}
we have discussed so far.

Proposition 2
.

Suppose
f_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}
and
\bar{\theta}_{n}
is a consistent estimator of
\theta_{0}
. Then
\tilde{g}_{n}=

\widehat{g}_{\bar{\theta}_{n}}
,
\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}\pm\mathord{\cdot})
,
\tilde{g}_{n}^{geo,sym}
,
{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}(\bar{\theta}_{n}\pm\mathord{\cdot})
, and
\tilde{g}_{n}^{sym,sm}

satisfy Condition
1
.

The key step in proving Proposition
2
is showing that the
L_{1}
consistency in Condition
1
(A) holds, which is established by Lemma
B.1
. The proof of
Lemma
B.1
can be found in Appendix
B.1
.

Lemma B.1
.

Suppose
\bar{\theta}_{n}\to_{p}\theta_{0}
, and
\tilde{g}_{n}
is one among

{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}(\bar{\theta}_{n}\pm\mathord{\cdot})
,
\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}\pm\mathord{\cdot})
,
\tilde{g}_{n}^{sym,sm}
,
\tilde{g}_{n}^{geo,sym}
, and
\widehat{g}_{\bar{\theta}_{n}}
. Then
\|\tilde{g}_{n}-g_{0}\|_{1}\to_{p}0
.

Now we are ready to prove Proposition
2
.

Proof of Proposition
2
.

As in the proof of Lemma
B.1
, one can show that it suffices to prove Proposition
2
when
\bar{\theta}_{n}\to_{a.s.}\theta_{0}
, and
y_{n}\to_{a.s.}0
. Hence, in what follows, we assume that
\bar{\theta}_{n}\to_{a.s.}\theta_{0}
, and
y_{n}\to_{a.s.}0
.
First we will verify Condition
1
when
\tilde{g}_{n}\in\mathcal{LC}
. Note that, this covers the case of
\widehat{g}_{\bar{\theta}_{n}}
,
\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}\pm\mathord{\cdot})
,
\tilde{g}_{n}^{geo,sym}
, and
{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}(\bar{\theta}_{n}\pm\mathord{\cdot})
. We will consider the case of
\tilde{g}_{n}^{sym,sm}
separately because the latter is not log-concave.

Assuming
\tilde{g}_{n}\in\mathcal{LC}
, to verify part A of Condition
1
, we first note that

\displaystyle\|\tilde{g}_{n}(\cdot+y_{n})-{g}_{0}\|_{1}\leq\|\tilde{g}_{n}(\cdot+y_{n})-{g}_{0}(\cdot+y_{n})\|_{1}+\|{g}_{0}(\cdot+y_{n})-{g}_{0}\|_{1},

whose first term converges to zero almost surely by
Lemma
B.1
.
Also, since
{g}_{0}
is continuous,
{g}_{0}(x+y_{n})
converges to
{g}_{0}(x)
for each
x\in\mathbb{R}
.
Therefore the second term also converges to zero almost surely by Glick’s Theorem
(
Devroye 1987
, Theorem 2.6,)
.
Thus we obtain that
\|\tilde{g}_{n}(\cdot+y_{n})-{g}_{0}\|_{1}\to_{a.s.}0
.
Since
\tilde{g}_{n}(\mathord{\cdot}+y_{n})
is log-concave, the above, combined with Proposition 2(c) of
Cule and Samworth 2010
, yields that

\|\tilde{g}_{n}(\cdot+y_{n})-{g}_{0}\|_{\infty}\to_{a.s.}0
which completes the verification of part A of Condition
1
.
As a consequence,

\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x+y_{n})=\log(\hn(x+y_n))\to_{a.s.}{\psi}_{0}(x),\quad\text{ for each }x\in\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}({\psi}_{0})).

Since
\tilde{\psi}_{n}
is concave for
\tilde{g}_{n}\in\mathcal{LC}
, Theorem 10.8 of
Rockafellar 1970
entails that the above
pointwise convergence translates to uniform convergence on all compact sets inside
\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}({\psi}_{0}))
, which leads to

\sup_{x\in K}|\tilde{\psi}_{n}(x+y_{n})-{\psi}_{0}(x)|\to_{a.s.}0,

proving part B of Condition
1
.
Since
\tilde{\psi}_{n}
is concave, Part C follows directly from Part B by Theorem 25.7 of
Rockafellar 1970
.
Thus we have established Condition
1
for
\tilde{g}_{n}=\widehat{g}_{\bar{\theta}_{n}}
,
\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}\pm\mathord{\cdot})
,
\tilde{g}_{n}^{geo,sym}
,
and
{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}(\bar{\theta}_{n}\pm\mathord{\cdot})
.

Now we verify Condition
1
for
\tilde{g}_{n}^{sym,sm}
.
Part A of Condition
1
can be verified noting (
12
) implies

\displaystyle 2\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}|{\tilde{g}_{n}^{sym,sm}}(x+y_{n})-{g}_{0}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}|\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x+y_{n})-{g}_{0}(x)|+\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}|\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x+y_{n})-{g}_{0}(x)|,

which converges to zero almost surely because, as we have already shown, the log-concave density
{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}
satisfies Condition
1
(A).

To prove part B,
we observe that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in K}\bigg|\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{sym,sm}(x+y_{n})-{\psi}_{0}(x)\bigg|\leq\dfrac{\sup_{x\in K}|{\tilde{g}_{n}^{sym,sm}}(x+y_{n})-{g}_{0}(x)|}{\min\bigg(\inf\limits_{x\in K}{\tilde{g}_{n}^{sym,sm}}(x+y_{n}),\inf\limits_{x\in K}{g}_{0}(x)\bigg)},

whose numerator converges to zero almost surely by part A of Condition
1
. Thus, to verify part B of Condition
1
for
{\tilde{g}_{n}^{sym,sm}}
, we only need to show that the denominator of the term on the right hand side of last display is bounded away from zero. To this end, notice that

\inf_{x\in K}{\tilde{g}_{n}^{sym,sm}}(x+y_{n})\geq\inf_{x\in K}\min({\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x+y_{n}),{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x-y_{n}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\to_{a.s.}}}\inf_{x\in K}g_{0}(x),

where (a) follows because we just showed that
{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}(\bar{\theta}_{n}\pm\mathord{\cdot})
satisfy Condition
1
. Now
\inf_{x\in K}g_{0}(x)>0
because
K
is a subset of
\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}({\psi}_{0}))
. Thus we have verified part B of Condition
1
for
{\tilde{g}_{n}^{sym,sm}}
.

Next note that
{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}
is a smooth function, and it is also positive on
\mathbb{R}
. Therefore
\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{sym,sm}
and
\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{sm}
are differentiable on
\mathbb{R}
. Therefore, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

(\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{sym,sm})^{\prime}(x)=\varrho_{n}(x)\bigg((\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{sm})^{\prime}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)\bigg)-(1-\varrho_{n}(x))\bigg((\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{sm})^{\prime}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)\bigg),

(56)

where
\varrho_{n}(x)={\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)/2\tilde{g}_{n}^{sym,sm}(x)<1
.
Thus

\displaystyle|(\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{sym,sm})^{\prime}(x+y_{n})-{\psi}_{0}^{\prime}(x)|\leq\varrho_{n}(x+y_{n})|(\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{sm})^{\prime}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x+y_{n})-{\psi}_{0}^{\prime}(x)|

\displaystyle\ +\ (1-\varrho_{n}(x+y_{n}))|(\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{sm})^{\prime}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x-y_{n})-{\psi}_{0}^{\prime}(-x)|.

Since
\varrho_{n}
is uniformly bounded by one, Condition
1
(C) applied on the concave function
\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{sm}(\bar{\theta}_{n}\pm\mathord{\cdot})
completes the verification of part C for
\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{sym,sm}
.
∎

B.1
Auxiliary lemmas for the proof of proposition
1

Proof of Lemma
B.1
.

First we show that it suffices to prove the current lemma when
\bar{\theta}_{n}\to_{a.s.}\theta_{0}
. Since
\bar{\theta}_{n}
is consistent, Fact
6
implies
given any subsequence of
\{\bar{\theta}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
, there exists a further subsequence
\{\overline{\theta}_{n_{k}}\}_{k\geq 1}
such that
\overline{\theta}_{n_{k}}\to_{a.s.}\theta_{0}
as
k\to\infty
. If we can show Therefore, along this subsequence
\{n_{k}\}_{k\geq 1}
, the
L_{1}
distance between
\tilde{g}_{n}
and
g_{0}
approaches zero almost surely. In that case, Fact
7
implies that
\|\tilde{g}_{n}-g_{0}\|_{1}
converges in probability to zero. Therefore, in what follows, we assume that
\bar{\theta}_{n}\to_{a.s.}\theta_{0}
.

We begin with the case of
{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}
.
Theorem 1 of
Chen and Samworth 2013
implies that when
f_{0}
has finite second central moment, we have

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}(x)-{f}_{0}(x)|dx\to_{a.s.}0.

(57)

That
f_{0}
has second central moment is immediate by Fact
1
.
Note that

\|{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+\cdot)-g_{0}\|_{1}\leq\|{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}-f_{0}\|_{1}+\|f_{0}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+\cdot)-g_{0}\|_{1},

whose first term converges to zero almost surely by (
57
), and the second term

\|f_{0}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+\cdot)-g_{0}\|_{1}=\|g_{0}(-\tilde{\delta}_{n}+\cdot)-g_{0}\|_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{2}H(g_{0}(-\tilde{\delta}_{n}+\cdot),g_{0})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\lesssim}}\tilde{\delta}_{n}\to_{a.s.}0

where (a) and (b) follow from Fact
5
and Fact
15
, respectively. Thus we have established that
\|{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+\cdot)-g_{0}\|_{1}\to_{a.s.}0
. Since
g_{0}
is symmetric about zero,
\|{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-\cdot)-g_{0}\|_{1}\to_{a.s.}0
follows.
Because
\|\widehat{h}_{n}-f_{0}\|_{1}\to_{a.s.}0
by Theorem
4
of
Cule and Samworth 2010
, the proof of

\|\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}\pm\mathord{\cdot})-g_{0}\|_{1}\to_{a.s.}0
follows in the same way.

The
L_{1}
consistency of
{\tilde{g}_{n}^{sym,sm}}
also follows noting (
12
) implies

\displaystyle 2\|{\tilde{g}_{n}^{sym,sm}}-{g}_{0}\|_{1}\leq\|{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+\cdot)-{g}_{0}\|_{1}+\|{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-\cdot)-{g}_{0}\|_{1}\to_{a.s.}0.

(58)

Next, we consider the geometric mean estimator
\widehat{f}_{n}^{geo,sym}
.
We have already established

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}\pm x)-{g}_{0}(x)|dx\to_{a.s.}0,

(59)

which entails that the distribution functions of
\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}\pm\mathord{\cdot})
converge weakly to
G_{0}
. The above, combined with
Proposition
2
(b) of
Cule and Samworth 2010
shows that (
59
) leads to almost sure convergence of

\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}\pm x)
to
{g}_{0}(x)
almost everywhere on
\mathbb{R}
with respect to the Lebesgue measure. As a consequence, it follows that

\widehat{f}_{n}^{geo,sym}(x)C^{geo}_{n}\to_{a.s.}\sqrt{{g}_{0}(x){g}_{0}(-x)}={g}_{0}(x)\quad a.e.\ \ x.

Recall from (
9
) that

C^{geo}_{n}=\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sqrt{\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)}dx.

From Scheffé’s Lemma it follows that
C^{geo}_{n}\to_{a.s.}\int d\tilde{G}_{0}=1
.
We have thus established that
\widehat{f}_{n}^{geo,sym}
converges almost everywhere to
{g}_{0}
almost surely. Therefore,
\tilde{G}^{sym,sm}_{n}
converges weakly to
G_{0}
almost surely. The desired strong
L_{1}
consistency then follows from Proposition 2(c) of
Cule and Samworth 2010
.

To establish the
L_{1}
consistency of the partial MLE estimator
\widehat{g}_{\bar{\theta}_{n}}
, we appeal to the projection theory developed in
Xu and Samworth 2019
. According to this theory,
\widehat{G}_{\bar{\theta}_{n}}
can be interpreted as the the projection (w.r.t. Kullback-Leibler divergence) of
{\mathbb{F}}_{n,X-\bar{\theta}_{n}}
, the empirical distribution function of the
X_{i}-\bar{\theta}_{n}
’s, onto the space of the distribution functions with density in
\mathcal{SLC}_{0}
. This projection operator has some continuity properties. In particular, if we can show that

d_{W}({\mathbb{F}}_{n,X-\bar{\theta}_{n}},G_{0})\to_{a.s.}0,

(60)

the desired
L_{1}
consistency
\|\widehat{g}_{\bar{\theta}_{n}}-{g}_{0}\|_{1}\to_{a.s.}0
follows from Proposition
6
of
Xu and Samworth 2019
provided
G_{0}
is non-degenerate and it has first finite moment. The non-degeneracy is trivial and the existence of first moment follows from Fact
1
. Hence, it is enough to prove (
60
) holds, for which, by Theorem
6.9
of
Villani 2009
,
it suffices to show

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|x|d{\mathbb{F}}_{n,X-\bar{\theta}_{n}}(x)\to_{a.s.}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|x|dG_{0}(x),

(61)

and that
{\mathbb{F}}_{n,X-\bar{\theta}_{n}}
converges to
G_{0}
weakly with probability one.
Since
\bar{\theta}_{n}
is strongly consistent for
\theta_{0}
, and

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|x|d{\mathbb{F}}_{n,X-\bar{\theta}_{n}}(x)=\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|x-\bar{\theta}_{n}|d\mathbb{F}_{n}(x),

for any
d>0
, an application of Glivenko-Cantelli Theorem
(
Van der Vaart and Wellner 1996
, for example, see Theorem
2.4.1
of)
yields

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{\bar{\theta}_{n}\in[\theta_{0}-d,\theta+d]}\bigg|\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|x-\bar{\theta}_{n}|d(\mathbb{F}_{n}-F_{0})(x)\bigg|\to_{a.s.}0.

On the other hand, strong consistency of
\bar{\theta}_{n}
implies
|x-\bar{\theta}_{n}|\leq|x-\theta_{0}|+1
with probability one for all sufficiently large
n
, where the latter is integrable with respect to
f_{0}
. Therefore, the dominated convergence theorem leads to

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|x-\bar{\theta}_{n}|dF_{0}(x)\to_{a.s.}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|x-\theta_{0}|dF_{0}(x)=\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|x|dG_{0}(x),

which proves (
61
).

Our next step is to prove the weak convergence of
{\mathbb{F}}_{n,X-\bar{\theta}_{n}}
to
G_{0}
. To this end, we note that

{\mathbb{F}}_{n,X-\bar{\theta}_{n}}(x)=\mathbb{F}_{n}(x+\bar{\theta}_{n})=\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}1_{(-\infty,x+\bar{\theta}_{n}]}(z)d\mathbb{F}_{n}(z),

which converges almost surely to

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}1_{(-\infty,x+\theta_{0}]}(z)dF_{0}(z)=G_{0}(x)

by an application of basic Glivenko-Cantelli Theorem
(
Van der Vaart and Wellner 1996
, see Theorem
2.4.1
of)
, and the fact that
F_{0}(x+\bar{\theta}_{n})\to_{a.s.}F_{0}(x+\theta_{0})
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
. This establishes (
60
), which proves the strong
L_{1}
consistency of
\widehat{g}_{\bar{\theta}_{n}}
, thus finishing the proof of the current lemma.

∎

B.1.1
Lemmas on Hellinger error of
\widehat{g}_{\bar{\theta}_{n}}
and
\tilde{g}_{n}^{geo,sym}
:

Lemma B.2
.

Suppose
f_{0}=g_{0}(\mathord{\cdot}-\theta_{0})
where
g_{0}\in\mathcal{SLC}_{0}
and
\bar{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
. Then

H(\widehat{g}_{\bar{\theta}_{n}},g_{0})=O_{p}(n^{-1/4})
.

Proof of Lemma
B.2
.

From Theorem 4.1 of
Doss and Wellner 2019b
it follows that
H(\widehat{g}_{\theta_{0}},g_{0})=O_{p}(n^{-2/5})
. The result will therefore follow by triangle inequality if we can show that

H(\widehat{g}_{\theta_{0}},\widehat{g}_{\bar{\theta}_{n}})=O_{p}(n^{-1/4})
.
To that end, for any function
\phi:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
, and distribution function
G
, we define the functional
\Phi:(\phi,G)\mapsto\mathbb{R}
by

\Phi(\phi,G)=\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\phi(x)dG(x)-\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{\phi(x)}dx.

(62)

Recall that we defined
\widehat{\psi}_{\theta}
to be
\log\widehat{g}_{\theta}
for any
\theta>0
.
Denoting
\mathbb{F}_{n,Y}
to be the empirical distribution function of random variables
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{n}
, we observe that for any
\theta>0
,
\widehat{\psi}_{\theta}
writes as
(
Doss and Wellner 2019b
, see (2.4) of )

\widehat{\psi}_{\theta}=\argmax_{\phi\in\mathcal{SC}_{0}}\Phi(\phi,\mathbb{F}_{n,X-\theta})=\argmax_{\phi\in\mathcal{SC}_{0}}\Phi(\phi,\mathbb{F}_{n,Z+\theta_{0}-\theta}),

where
Z=X-\theta_{0}
. Let us denote
\delta=\theta_{0}-\theta
.
Using Lemma
B.4
we obtain that

\argmax_{\phi\in\mathcal{SC}_{0}}\Phi(\phi,\mathbb{F}_{n,Z+\delta})=\argmax_{\phi\in\mathcal{C}}\Phi(\phi,\mathbb{F}^{sym}_{n,Z+\delta}),\quad\text{ where }

\mathbb{F}^{sym}_{n,Z+\delta}(x)=\frac{\mathbb{F}_{n,Z+\delta}(x)+1-\mathbb{F}_{n,Z+\delta}(-x)}{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\frac{\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(x-\delta)+1-\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(-x-\delta)}{2}

is the symmetrized version of
\mathbb{F}_{n,Z+\delta}
. Here (a) follows because
\mathbb{F}_{n,Z+\delta}(x)
equals
\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(x-\delta)
.
In particular, the choice
\theta=\theta_{0}
yields
\delta=0
, which leads to

\widehat{\psi}_{\theta_{0}}=\argmax_{\phi\in\mathcal{C}}\Phi(\phi,\mathbb{F}^{sym}_{n,Z})
,
where

\mathbb{F}^{sym}_{n,Z}(x)=(\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(x)+1-\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(-x))/{2}.

When
\theta=\bar{\theta}_{n}
, on the other hand,
\delta=\tilde{\delta}_{n}
, which yields

\widehat{\psi}_{\bar{\theta}_{n}}=\argmax_{\phi\in\mathcal{C}}\Phi(\phi,\mathbb{F}^{sym}_{n,Z+\tilde{\delta}_{n}}),\ \mathbb{F}^{sym}_{n,Z+\tilde{\delta}_{n}}(x)=\frac{\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(x-\tilde{\delta}_{n})+1-\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(-x-\tilde{\delta}_{n})}{2}.

If we can show that
\mathbb{F}^{sym}_{n,Z}(x)
and
\mathbb{F}^{sym}_{n,Z+\tilde{\delta}_{n}}
are non-degenerate with finite first moment, then Theorem 2 of
Barber and Samworth 2020
would imply that

H(\widehat{g}_{\bar{\theta}_{n}},\widehat{g}_{\theta_{0}})\leq C\bigg(\frac{d_{W}(\mathbb{F}^{sym}_{n,Z},\mathbb{F}^{sym}_{n,Z+\tilde{\delta}_{n}})}{\epsilon_{\mathbb{F}^{sym}_{n,Z}}}\bigg)^{1/2},

(63)

where
C>0
is an absolute constant and for any distribution function
F
,
\epsilon_{F}
is defined by

\epsilon_{F}=E_{F}[|Y-E_{F}[Y]|].

Here
E_{F}
is the expectation with respect to
F
.
Since
G_{0}
is non-degenerate,
\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}
is non-degenerate with probability one. Therefore both
\mathbb{F}^{sym}_{n,Z}(x)
and
\mathbb{F}^{sym}_{n,Z+\tilde{\delta}_{n}}
are non-degenerate with probabilty one. Also, because
\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}
has finite first moment for all
n\geq 1
, both
\mathbb{F}^{sym}_{n,Z}(x)
and
\mathbb{F}^{sym}_{n,Z+\tilde{\delta}_{n}}
have finite first moment for all
n\geq 1
. Therefore (
63
) holds.

Next, we show that
\epsilon_{\mathbb{F}^{sym}_{n,Z}}
is bounded away from zero almost surely. To that end, we first prove the side result that
d_{W}(\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}^{sym},G_{0})\to_{a.s.}0
.
By (
17
),

\displaystyle d_{W}(\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}^{sym},G_{0})=

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}^{sym}(x)-G_{0}(x)|dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left|\frac{\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(x)+1-\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(-x)}{2}-G_{0}(x)\right|,

which, due to the symmetry of
g_{0}
about the origin, equals

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left|\frac{\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(x)+1-\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(-x)}{2}-\frac{G_{0}(x)+1-G_{0}(-x)}{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{2}\bigg(\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left|\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(x)-G_{0}(x)\right|dx+\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left|\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(-x)-G_{0}(-x)\right|dx\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left|\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(x)-G_{0}(x)\right|dx,

which equals
d_{W}(\mathbb{F}_{n,Z},G_{0})
. The latter converges to zero almost surely by Varadarajan’s Theorem
(
Dudley 2003
, Theorem 11.4.1)
and the strong law of large numbers. Therefore,
d_{W}(\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}^{sym},G_{0})\to_{a.s.}0
follows.

Proposition 1 of
Barber and Samworth 2020
implies that if
F
and
F^{\prime}
are distribution functions with finite first moment, then
\epsilon_{F}>0
, and
|\epsilon_{F}-\epsilon_{F^{\prime}}|
is bounded by
2d_{W}(F,F^{\prime})
. Now
G_{0}
being log-concave, has finite first moment. Therefore we have
\epsilon_{G_{0}}>0
. Also since
G_{0}
and
\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}^{sym}
are non-degenerate, it follows that

\absolutevalue{\epsilon_{\mathbb F_{n,Z}^{sym}}-\epsilon_{G_0}}\leq d_{W}({\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}^{sym}},G_{0}),

which implies

\epsilon_{\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}^{sym}}>\epsilon_{G_{0}}-2d_{W}({\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}^{sym}},G_{0})
. We have just shown
d_{W}(\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}^{sym},G_{0})
converges to zero almost surely. Therefore
\epsilon_{\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}^{sym}}>\epsilon_{G_{0}}/2
for sufficiently large
n
almost surely.

If we can show that
d_{W}(\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}^{sym},\mathbb{F}_{n,Z+\tilde{\delta}_{n}}^{sym})=O_{p}(\tilde{\delta}_{n})
, the proof of lemma
B.2
follows from (
63
) because
\tilde{\delta}_{n}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
. To that end, we use an alternative representation of
d_{W}
which is due to the Kantorovich-Rubinstein duality theorem
(
Bobkov and Ledoux 2014
, cf. Theorem 2.5)
. For distribution functions
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
with finite first moment, it holds that

d_{W}(F_{1},F_{2})=\sup_{h\in\text{Lip}_{1}}\absolutevalue{\edint h(x)d(F_1-F_2)}

where
\text{Lip}_{1}
is the set of all real-valued functions
h:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
with Lipschitz constant one.
Therefore,

\displaystyle d_{W}(\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}^{sym},\mathbb{F}_{n,Z+\tilde{\delta}_{n}}^{sym})=

\displaystyle\ \sup_{h\in\text{Lip}_{1}}\frac{1}{2}\bigg|\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}h(x)d(\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(x-\tilde{\delta}_{n})+\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(-x-\tilde{\delta}_{n}))

\displaystyle\ -\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}h(x)d(\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(x)+\mathbb{G}_{n}(-x)\bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sup_{h\in{\text{Lip}}_{1}}\frac{1}{2}\bigg|\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Big(h(x+\tilde{\delta}_{n})-h(x)\Big)d(\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(x)+\mathbb{G}_{n}(-x))\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \sup_{h\in{\text{Lip}}_{1}}\frac{1}{2}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|h(x+\tilde{\delta}_{n})-h(x)|d(\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(x)+\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(-x))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\ |\tilde{\delta}_{n}|\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d(\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(x)+\mathbb{F}_{n,Z}(-x))dx/2,

which equals
|\tilde{\delta}_{n}|
. Here (a) uses the fact that
h
is Lipschitz with Lipschitz constant one. Therefore, the proof follows.
∎

Lemma B.3
.

Suppose
f_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}
and
\bar{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{0}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
. Then the geometric mean estimator
\tilde{g}_{n}(x)=\sqrt{\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)}/C_{n}^{geo}
satisfies
H(\tilde{g}_{n},g_{0})=O_{p}(n^{-2/5})
.

Proof of Lemma
B.3
.

We first decompose
H(\tilde{g}_{n},g_{0})^{2}
as follows:

\displaystyle H(\tilde{g}_{n},g_{0})^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left(\left(\sqrt{\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)}/C_{n}^{geo}\right)^{1/2}-\sqrt{g_{0}(x)}\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ 2(C_{n}^{geo})^{-1}\underbrace{\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left(\left(\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)\right)^{1/4}-\sqrt{g_{0}(x)}\right)^{2}dx}_{T_{1}}

\displaystyle\ +2(C_{n}^{geo})^{-1}\underbrace{(\sqrt{C_{n}^{geo}}-1)^{2}}_{T_{2}}.

(64)

We focus on
T_{1}
first. Note that

\displaystyle T_{1}=

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left(\left(\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)\right)^{1/4}-\sqrt{g_{0}(x)}\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\left(\left(\frac{\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)}{\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)}\right)^{1/4}\sqrt{\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)}-\sqrt{g_{0}(x)}\right)^{2}

(65)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\ \underbrace{H(\widehat{h}_{n},f_{0}(\mathord{\cdot}+\tilde{\delta}_{n}))^{2}}_{T_{11}}+\underbrace{\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\bigg(\left(\frac{\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)}{\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)}\right)^{1/4}-1\bigg)^{2}\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)dx}_{T_{12}}.

T_{11}
is bounded by
2H(\widehat{h}_{n},f_{0})^{2}+2H(f_{0},f_{0}(\mathord{\cdot}+\tilde{\delta}_{n}))^{2}
. Thus
T_{11}=O_{p}(n^{-4/5})
follows noting (a)
H(\widehat{h}_{n},f_{0})=O_{p}(n^{-2/5})
by Theorem 3.2 of
Doss and Wellner 2016
, and (b)
H(f_{0},f_{0}(\mathord{\cdot}+\tilde{\delta}_{n}))=O_{p}(\tilde{\delta}_{n})
by Fact
15
.
Since
(x-1)^{2}\leq(x^{2}-1)^{2}
for
x>0
, the term
T_{12}
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\bigg(\sqrt{\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)}-\sqrt{\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)}\bigg)^{2}dx=\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\bigg(\sqrt{\widehat{h}_{n}(x)}-\sqrt{\widehat{h}_{n}(2\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)}\bigg)^{2}dx.

Since
f_{0}(2\theta_{0}-x)=f_{0}(x)
, we can further bound
T_{12}
by a constant multiple of

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Big(\sqrt{\widehat{h}_{n}(x)}-\sqrt{f_{0}(x)}\Big)^{2}dx+\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Big(\sqrt{\widehat{h}_{n}(2\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)}-\sqrt{f_{0}(2\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)}\Big)^{2}dx

\displaystyle\ +\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Big(\sqrt{f_{0}(2\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)}-\sqrt{f_{0}(2\theta_{0}-x)}\Big)^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ 4H(\widehat{h}_{n},f_{0})^{2}+\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Big(\sqrt{f_{0}(x-2\tilde{\delta}_{n})}-\sqrt{f_{0}(x)}\Big)^{2}dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 8H(\widehat{h}_{n},f_{0})^{2}+4H(f_{0}(\mathord{\cdot}+2\tilde{\delta}_{n}),f_{0})^{2},

whose first term is
O_{p}(n^{-4/5})
, and the second term, by Fact
15
, is of order
O_{p}(\tilde{\delta}_{n}^{2})
. Thus similar to
T_{11}
,
T_{12}
is
O_{p}(n^{-4/5})
as well. Therefore from (
B.1.1
) it follows that
T_{1}=O_{p}(n^{-4/5})
.

Using the fact that
(x-1)^{2}\leq(x^{2}-1)^{2}
for non-negative
x
, we obtain that

T_{2}\leq(C_{n}^{geo}-1)^{2}
, which equals

\displaystyle\bigg(\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sqrt{\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)}dx-1\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \bigg(\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sqrt{\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)}\Big(\sqrt{\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)}-\sqrt{\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)}\Big)dx\bigg)^{2}.

The Cauchy-Schwarz inequality implies that the term on the right hand side of the above display is bounded by

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Big(\sqrt{\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)}-\sqrt{\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)}\Big)^{2}dx\lesssim\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Big(\sqrt{\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)}-\sqrt{f_{0}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)}\Big)^{2}dx

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Big(\sqrt{\widehat{h}_{n}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)}-\sqrt{f_{0}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)}\Big)^{2}dx+\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Big(\sqrt{f_{0}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)}-\sqrt{f_{0}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)}\Big)^{2}dx.

Clearly, the first two terms equal
4H(\widehat{h}_{n},f_{0})^{2}
, which is
O_{p}(n^{-4/5})
. Since
f_{0}
is symmetric about
\theta_{0}
, we can show that
f_{0}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)=f_{0}(2\tilde{\delta}_{n}+\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)
, which implies the third term equals
H(f_{0},f_{0}(\mathord{\cdot}+2\tilde{\delta}_{n}))^{2}
, which,
by Fact
15
, is of order
O_{p}(\tilde{\delta}_{n}^{2})
. Thus we have established that
T_{2}
is
O_{p}(n^{-4/5})
as well, which also implies that
C_{n}^{geo}\to_{p}1
. Therefore, by Slutskey’s Theorem and (
B.1.1
), the proof follows.
∎

Lemma B.4
.

Suppose
F
is non-degenerate and
F
has finite first moment.
Define

F^{sym}_{\theta}(x)=2^{-1}\bigg(F(x)+1-F(2\theta-x)\bigg).

Then it follows that

\argmax_{\phi\in\mathcal{SC}_{\theta}}\Phi(\phi,F)=\argmax_{\phi\in\mathcal{C}}\Phi(\phi,F^{sym}_{\theta})

where
\Phi
is as defined in (
62
).

Proof of Lemma
B.4
.

First we will show that

\displaystyle\argmax_{\phi\in\mathcal{SC}_{\theta}}\Phi(\phi,F)=\argmax_{\phi\in\mathcal{SC}_{\theta}}\Phi(\phi,F^{sym}_{\theta}).

(66)

Recall the definition of
\Psi
from (
13
).
For any distribution function
F
and
\psi\in\mathcal{SC}_{0}
, the following holds:

\displaystyle\Psi(0,\psi,F)=

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}\psi(x)dF(x)+\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\psi(x)dF(x)-\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{\psi(x)}dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\psi(-x)dF(-x)+\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\psi(x)dF(x)-\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{\psi(x)}dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\psi(x)d(F(x)-F(-x))-\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{\psi(x)}dx.

where the last step uses
\psi(x)=\psi(-x)
. By symmetry, it also follows that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\psi(x)d(F(x)-F(-x))=\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}\psi(x)d(F(x)-F(-x)).

\text{Therefore,}\quad\Psi(0,\psi,F)=2^{-1}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi(x)d(F(x)-F(-x))-\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{\psi(x)}dx.

Equation
13
implies

\Psi(\theta,\psi,F)=\Psi(\theta,\psi,F(\cdot+\theta))
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\Psi(\theta,\psi,F)=

\displaystyle\ 2^{-1}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi(x)d(F(\theta+x)-F(\theta-x))-\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{\psi(x)}dx

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ 2^{-1}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi(z-\theta)d(F(z)-F(2\theta-z))-\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{\psi(z)}dz

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\psi(z-\theta)dF^{sym}_{\theta}(z)-\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{\psi(z)}dz=\Psi(\theta,\psi,F^{sym}_{\theta})

(67)

where (a) follows substituting
\theta+x
by
z
.
Suppose
\psi\in\mathcal{SC}_{0}
and
\phi=\psi(\cdot-\theta)
. Equation
62
implies that for any
\phi\in\mathcal{SC}_{\theta}
,

\Phi(\phi,F)=\Psi(\theta,\psi,F)
, where
\psi=\phi(\cdot+\theta)
.
This, in conjunction with (
B.1.1
), yields that

\Phi(\phi,F)=\Phi(\phi,F^{sym}_{\theta})
for any
\phi\in\mathcal{SC}_{\theta}
.
Therefore, (
66
) follows.

Proposition 4(iii) of
Xu and Samworth 2019
entails that
\argmax_{\phi\in\mathcal{SC}_{\theta}}\Phi(\phi,F)
exists and is unique for a degenerate
F
with finite first moment. Under similar conditions on
F
,
\argmax_{\phi\in\mathcal{C}}\Phi(\phi,F^{sym}_{\theta})
also exists and it is unique by Theorem 2.7 of
Dümbgen et al. 2011
. Therefore, it suffices to prove
\argmax_{\phi\in\mathcal{C}}\Phi(\phi,F^{sym}_{\theta})
is in
\mathcal{SC}_{\theta}
because the latter implies

\argmax_{\phi\in\mathcal{SC}_{\theta}}\Phi(\phi,F^{sym}_{\theta})=\argmax_{\phi\in\mathcal{C}}\Phi(\phi,F^{sym}_{\theta}),

which, in conjuction with (
66
), completes the proof of the current lemma.

Without loss of generality, we will assume
\theta=0
.
In that case,
F^{sym}_{0}(x)=(F(x)+1-F(-x))/2
, which implies

dF^{sym}_{0}(x)=(dF(x)-dF(-x))/2=-dF^{sym}_{0}(-x).

(68)

For any concave function
\phi\in\mathcal{C}
, note that
\Phi(\phi,F^{sym}_{0})
can be written as

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}\phi(x)dF^{sym}_{0}(x)+\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\phi(x)dF^{sym}_{0}(x)-\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{\phi(x)}dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\phi(-x)dF^{sym}_{0}(-x)+\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\phi(x)dF^{sym}_{0}(x)-\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{\phi(x)}dx

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\phi(-x)dF^{sym}_{0}(x)+\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\phi(x)dF^{sym}_{0}(x)-\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{\phi(x)}dx

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\dfrac{\phi(-x)+\phi(x)}{2}dF^{sym}_{0}(x)-\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{\phi(x)}dx,

where (a) uses (
68
), and (b) follows since

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\dfrac{\phi(-x)+\phi(x)}{2}dF^{sym}_{0}(x)=\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}\dfrac{\phi(-x)+\phi(x)}{2}dF^{sym}_{0}(x)

by symmetry.
Moreover, since exponential function is convex, we obtain

\Phi(\phi,F_{0}^{sym})\leq\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\dfrac{\phi(x)+\phi(-x)}{2}dF^{sym}_{0}(x)-\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{(\phi(x)+\phi(-x))/2}dx,

which proves that a
\phi\in\mathcal{SC}_{0}
maximizes
\Phi(\phi,F^{sym}_{0})
over
\mathcal{C}
, as speculated.
Therefore, the proof follows.

∎

C
Proof of Theorem
2

Similar to Theorem
1
, we can argue that it suffices to prove Theorem
2
for the case when
\eta_{n}
is
Cn^{-2p/5}
. For the rest of the proof, we will denote
\xi_{n}=(\tilde{G}^{sym,sm}_{n})^{-1}(1-\eta_{n})
.
First of all note that
{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}(\bar{\theta}_{n}\pm\mathord{\cdot})
and
\tilde{g}_{n}^{sym,sm}
satisfy Condition
1
by Proposition
2
. Lemma
C.1
in Appendix
C.1
implies that these densities also satisfy Condition
2
with
p=1/5
. Since the proof of Theorem
2
closely follows the proof of Theorem
1
, we will only highlight the differences. Following the arguments in Theorem
1
, we can represent
-(\tilde{\theta}_{n}-\bar{\theta}_{n})
as the sum of the three terms
T_{1n}
,
T_{2n}
, and
T_{5n}
, where

T_{1n}=\displaystyle\int_{-\xi_{n}}^{\xi_{n}}\dfrac{(\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{sym,sm})^{\prime}(z)-{\psi}_{0}^{\prime}(z-\tilde{\delta}_{n})}{\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})}d(\mathbb{F}_{n}(z+\bar{\theta}_{n})-F_{0}(z+\bar{\theta}_{n})),

T_{2n}=\displaystyle\int_{-\xi_{n}}^{\xi_{n}}\dfrac{(\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{sym,sm})^{\prime}(z)}{\widehat{\mathcal{I}}_{n}(\eta_{n})}\bigg(f_{0}(z+\bar{\theta}_{n})-g_{0}(z)\bigg)dz,

and
T_{5n}
is as in (
A
). The treatment of
T_{5n}
in this case will be identical to that in Theorem
1
. Hence it suffices to redo step one and step two of Theorem
1
only in the context of
\tilde{g}_{n}^{sym,sm}
.

Step one: showing
T_{1n}=o_{p}(1)
:

The main difference in the analysis of
T_{1n}
between Theorem
1
and here stems from the fact that
(\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{sym,sm})^{\prime}
is no longer guaranteed to be monotone since
\tilde{g}_{n}^{sym,sm}
is not log-concave. So one needs to be more careful before applying the Donsker theorem to control the
T_{1n}
term here. By construction,
{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}
and
\tilde{g}_{n}^{sym,sm}
are positive on the entire real line, and differentiable everywhere. Using
(
56
), we obtain the formula

(\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{sym,sm})^{\prime}(x)=\varrho_{n}(x)\bigg((\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{sm})^{\prime}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)\bigg)-(1-\varrho_{n}(x))\bigg((\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{sm})^{\prime}(\bar{\theta}_{n}-x)\bigg),

where
\varrho_{n}(x)={\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}(\bar{\theta}_{n}+x)/2\tilde{g}_{n}^{sym,sm}(x)
.
Note that
(\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{sm})^{\prime}(\bar{\theta}_{n}\pm\mathord{\cdot})
is non-increasing because
{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}
is log-concave. On the other hand, because
{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}
is smooth, and
{\widehat{h}_{n}^{sm}}>0
on
\mathbb{R}
,
\varrho_{n}
is differentiable with derivative
